            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def checkout_many(self, member_id, isbns, today):
        return [self.checkout(member_id, isbn, today) for isbn in isbns]
--- solution
from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def checkout_many(self, member_id, isbns, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        if len(set(isbns)) != len(isbns):
            raise LibraryError("the same book is listed twice")
        for isbn in isbns:
            self.book(isbn)
            if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
                raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
            if self.available(isbn) < 1:
                raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        if len(current) + len(isbns) > MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        due = today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS)
        new = [Loan(member_id, isbn, due) for isbn in isbns]
        self.loans.extend(new)
        return new
--- hint
The starter calls `checkout` once per book, so when the third book fails, the first two loans are already in the list. All-or-nothing means: check every book first, and only then add any loan.
--- hint
A set has no duplicates, so `len(set(isbns)) != len(isbns)` spots an ISBN listed twice. The limit check compares `len(current) + len(isbns)` with `MAX_LOANS`. Build all the new loans, then `self.loans.extend(...)` them in one step.
--- check case | Three books at once
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, i, "A")) for i in "123"], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout_many("a", ["2", "1"], date(2026, 3, 1)), len(lib.loans))[2:])(Library())
=> ([Loan("a", "2", date(2026, 3, 15)), Loan("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 15))], 2)
--- check test | One unavailable book means no loans at all
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, i, "A")) for i in "123"], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.add_member(Member("b", "Bo")), lib.checkout("b", "3", date(2026, 1, 1)), raises(LibraryError, lambda: lib.checkout_many("a", ["1", "2", "3"], date(2026, 1, 1))), lib.loans_of("a"))[-2:])(Library()) == (True, [])
--- check test | The limit counts the loans you already have
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, i, "A")) for i in "1234"], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 1, 1)), lib.checkout("a", "2", date(2026, 1, 1)), raises(LibraryError, lambda: lib.checkout_many("a", ["3", "4"], date(2026, 1, 1))), len(lib.loans), len(lib.checkout_many("a", ["3"], date(2026, 1, 1))))[-3:])(Library()) == (True, 2, 1)
--- check test | Duplicates, books you already have, unknown books and members
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, i, "A", 5)) for i in "12"], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 1, 1)), [raises(LibraryError, lambda x=x: lib.checkout_many(*x, date(2026, 1, 1))) for x in (("a", ["2", "2"]), ("a", ["2", "1"]), ("a", ["2", "9"]), ("zz", ["2"]))], len(lib.loans))[-2:])(Library()) == ([True, True, True, True], 1)
--- check test | An empty list changes nothing
(lambda lib: (lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout_many("a", [], date(2026, 1, 1)), lib.loans, raises(LibraryError, lambda: lib.checkout_many("zz", [], date(2026, 1, 1))))[1:])(Library()) == ([], [], True)

+++ practice | Debug: returning everything skips a book
--- task
`return_all(member_id)` should take every loan of one member out of `self.loans` and return them, in the order they were in the list. Other members' loans must stay, in their order.

It works when the member's loans are spread out. But when two of their loans sit next to each other in the list, only the first is returned, and the second stays behind. Fix it.
--- starter
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.loans = []

    def lend(self, member_id, isbn, due):
        self.loans.append(Loan(member_id, isbn, due))

    def return_all(self, member_id):
        returned = []
        for loan in self.loans:
            if loan.member_id == member_id:
                self.loans.remove(loan)
                returned.append(loan)
        return returned
--- solution
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.loans = []

    def lend(self, member_id, isbn, due):
        self.loans.append(Loan(member_id, isbn, due))

    def return_all(self, member_id):
        returned = [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]
        self.loans = [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id != member_id]
        return returned
--- hint
The loop walks the list by position. When `remove` takes out the item at the current position, every later item moves back one place. Which item does the loop look at next?
--- hint
Never change a list while a loop is walking it. Build the answer from the list first, then replace `self.loans` with a new list of the loans that stay.
--- check case | Two loans side by side are both returned
(lambda lib: ([lib.lend(m, i, date(2026, 1, 1)) for m, i in (("a", "1"), ("a", "2"), ("b", "3"), ("a", "4"))], [l.isbn for l in lib.return_all("a")], [l.isbn for l in lib.loans])[1:])(Library())
=> (["1", "2", "4"], ["3"])
--- check case | Other members' loans stay in order
(lambda lib: ([lib.lend(m, i, date(2026, 1, 1)) for m, i in (("b", "1"), ("a", "2"), ("a", "3"), ("c", "4"), ("b", "5"))], lib.return_all("a"), [l.isbn for l in lib.loans])[-1])(Library())
=> ["1", "4", "5"]
--- check test | A member with no loans
(lambda lib: (lib.lend("a", "1", date(2026, 1, 1)), lib.return_all("z"), len(lib.loans))[1:])(Library()) == ([], 1)

+++ practice | A waiting list
--- task
When every copy of a book is out, members can join a waiting list for it. The starter is the library from the lesson. Change and add:

- `reserve(member_id, isbn)` puts the member at the end of that book's waiting list. It raises `LibraryError` if the member or the book does not exist, if a copy is available right now, if the member already has the book on loan, or if they are already waiting for it.
- `waiting(isbn)` returns the member ids waiting for that book, first in line first (`[]` if nobody). An unknown ISBN raises `LibraryError`.
- `return_book(member_id, isbn, today)` now takes `today`. It removes the loan as before (raising `LibraryError` if there is none). Then, if anyone is waiting for that book, the first in line is taken off the list and gets a new loan of it, due `LOAN_DAYS` after `today`, and `return_book` returns their member id. If that member already has `MAX_LOANS` loans, they lose their place and the next in line is tried. If nobody gets the book, it returns `None`.
--- starter
from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def return_book(self, member_id, isbn):
        for i, loan in enumerate(self.loans):
            if loan.member_id == member_id and loan.isbn == isbn:
                return self.loans.pop(i)
        raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} has not borrowed {isbn}")
--- solution
from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []
        self.queues = {}

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def waiting(self, isbn):
        self.book(isbn)
        return list(self.queues.get(isbn, []))

    def reserve(self, member_id, isbn):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        if self.available(isbn) > 0:
            raise LibraryError(f"a copy of {isbn} is available")
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        queue = self.queues.setdefault(isbn, [])
        if member_id in queue:
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} is already waiting for {isbn}")
        queue.append(member_id)

    def take_loan(self, member_id, isbn):
        for i, loan in enumerate(self.loans):
            if loan.member_id == member_id and loan.isbn == isbn:
                return self.loans.pop(i)
        raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} has not borrowed {isbn}")

    def return_book(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        self.take_loan(member_id, isbn)
        queue = self.queues.get(isbn, [])
        while queue:
            first = queue.pop(0)
            if len(self.loans_of(first)) < MAX_LOANS:
                self.loans.append(Loan(first, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS)))
                return first
        return None
--- hint
Keep the waiting lists in a dict from ISBN to a list of member ids, created in `__init__`. `setdefault(isbn, [])` gives a book's list, making it the first time.
--- hint
`reserve` is all checks and then one `append`. `waiting` should return a copy of the list (`list(...)`), so a caller cannot change the real queue.
--- hint
In `return_book`, first remove the loan (raising if there is none). Then loop while the queue has people: `pop(0)` the first; if they have fewer than `MAX_LOANS` loans, add their loan and return their id. If the loop ends, return `None`.
--- check case | The next in line gets the book
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "Dune", "F")), [lib.add_member(Member(m, m)) for m in "abc"], lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.reserve("b", "1"), lib.reserve("c", "1"), lib.waiting("1"), lib.return_book("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 10)), lib.waiting("1"), lib.loans)[-4:])(Library())
=> (["b", "c"], "b", ["c"], [Loan("b", "1", date(2026, 3, 24))])
--- check test | Nobody waiting: the copy is simply free
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "Dune", "F")), lib.add_member(Member("a", "A")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.return_book("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 2)), lib.available("1"), lib.waiting("1"))[-3:])(Library()) == (None, 1, [])
--- check test | Reserving is refused when it makes no sense
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(b) for b in (Book("1", "Dune", "F"), Book("2", "Emma", "J"))], [lib.add_member(Member(m, m)) for m in "ab"], lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.reserve("b", "1"), [raises(LibraryError, lambda x=x: lib.reserve(*x)) for x in (("b", "2"), ("a", "1"), ("b", "1"), ("zz", "1"), ("b", "9"))], lib.waiting("1"), raises(LibraryError, lambda: lib.waiting("9")))[-3:])(Library()) == ([True, True, True, True, True], ["b"], True)
--- check test | A member at the loan limit loses their place
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, i, "A")) for i in "1234"], [lib.add_member(Member(m, m)) for m in "abc"], lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), [lib.checkout("b", i, date(2026, 3, 1)) for i in "234"], lib.reserve("b", "1"), lib.reserve("c", "1"), lib.return_book("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 5)), lib.waiting("1"), [l.isbn for l in lib.loans_of("c")])[-3:])(Library()) == ("c", [], ["1"])
--- check test | Returning a book you do not have still raises
raises(LibraryError, lambda: (lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "D", "F")), lib.add_member(Member("a", "A")), lib.return_book("a", "1", date(2026, 1, 1))))(Library()))

=== pyp-11 | Library system 3: saving and loading with JSON
--- teach
Last lesson the library learned to lend: loans with due dates, and rules checked before anything changes. But everything lives in the program's [[memory|memory-vs-disk]]. When the program stops, every book, member and loan is gone. A library that forgets everything overnight is not much use.

The data needs to be **persisted**: written down somewhere, as text in a file, and read back later. Think of the clerk copying every card into a notebook at closing time, and copying them back onto fresh cards in the morning. This lesson writes the notebook. It takes seven small steps.

**Step 1: meet JSON.** **[[JSON|json-history]]** is a way of writing data as plain text. It looks almost exactly like Python's dicts and lists, humans can read it, and nearly every programming language understands it. Python's `json` module turns data into JSON text and back:

```python
import json

text = json.dumps({"name": "Ada", "tags": ["x", "y"], "active": True})
print(text)          # {"name": "Ada", "tags": ["x", "y"], "active": true}

data = json.loads(text)
print(data["tags"])  # ['x', 'y']
```

`json.dumps` ("dump string") turns data into a string of JSON text. `json.loads` ("load string") reads JSON text and gives back dicts and lists. Notice one small change: Python's `True` is written `true` in JSON, and Python's `None` is written `null`.

**Step 2: what JSON can hold.** JSON only knows six kinds of thing: **dicts (with string keys), lists, strings, numbers, booleans and null**. A `Book` or a `date` is none of those. So anything else must be converted on the way out, and rebuilt on the way in:

| Python | Out (to JSON) | In (from JSON) |
|---|---|---|
| dataclass | `dataclasses.asdict(obj)` | `Book(**d)` |
| `date` | `d.isoformat()` gives `"2026-03-15"` | `date.fromisoformat(s)` |
| tuple, set | a list | rebuild if you need the type |

Here are the first two rows in action. `asdict` turns a dataclass into a dict of its fields. Going back, `Book(**d)` uses the double star from the `*args, **kwargs` lesson: it spreads the dict out into keyword arguments, so it means `Book(isbn="1", title="Dune", ...)`.

```python
from dataclasses import asdict

d = asdict(Book("1", "Dune", "Frank Herbert", 2))
print(d)          # {'isbn': '1', 'title': 'Dune', 'author': 'Frank Herbert', 'copies': 2}
print(Book(**d))  # Book(isbn='1', title='Dune', author='Frank Herbert', copies=2)
```

A date becomes text written [[year first|iso-dates]], and comes back as a real `date`:

```python
from datetime import date

print(date(2026, 3, 15).isoformat())           # 2026-03-15
print(repr(date.fromisoformat("2026-03-15")))  # datetime.date(2026, 3, 15)
```

**Step 3: design the file format on purpose.** Write down the shape of your save file, because other programs, and future versions of yours, will [[depend on it|format-promise]]. Here it is a dict of three lists:

```
{"books": [...], "members": [...], "loans": [...]}
```

Books and members are dicts of their fields. A loan is `{"member_id": ..., "isbn": ..., "due": "YYYY-MM-DD"}`, with its date as text.

**Step 4: make the output stable.** Two extra arguments to `json.dumps` make it write the same text for the same data, every time. `indent=2` puts each item on its own line, indented by two spaces. `sort_keys=True` writes the keys of every dict in A-to-Z order:

```python
print(json.dumps({"b": 2, "a": [1, 2]}, indent=2, sort_keys=True))
# {
#   "a": [
#     1,
#     2
#   ],
#   "b": 2
# }
```

Stable text is easy to read, and easy to [[compare with an older save|stable-diffs]].

**Step 5: build from the class.** Loading makes a brand-new library, so it cannot be an ordinary method: there is no library object yet to call it on. A **class method** is a method you call on the class itself. You write `@classmethod` above it, and its first parameter is the class, named **`cls`** by convention, instead of an object named `self`:

```python
class Library:
    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, text):
        library = cls()      # the same as Library(): a new, empty library
        ...                  # fill it in
        return library

lib = Library.from_json(text)
```

A method like this, whose job is to build a new object from something, is called an **[[alternative constructor|cls-not-self]]**. You have already used one: `date.fromisoformat` builds a `date` from text.

Fill the new library in by calling `add_book` and `add_member` with rebuilt objects, so that loaded data passes through the same rules as everything else.

**Step 6: the round trip is the test.** The one property that matters most for saving and loading is this: load what you saved, and you get the same library back. `Library.from_json(lib.to_json())` must behave exactly like `lib`. Testing that [[there and back|round-trip]] catches almost every mistake at once.

**Step 7: do not trust the file.** Code that reads a file crosses a **[[trust boundary|trust-boundary]]**: the data comes from outside your program. A save file can be cut off halfway, edited by hand, or written by an older version. Each kind of damage raises a different error:

- broken text makes `json.loads` raise `json.JSONDecodeError`, which is a kind of `ValueError`;
- a missing key, like `data["books"]` in `{}`, raises `KeyError`;
- a wrong or missing field makes building the dataclass raise `TypeError`;
- a bad date, like `date.fromisoformat("someday")`, raises `ValueError`.

The caller should not have to know all of these. So catch them all, and raise one clear `LibraryError("corrupt save file: ...")`:

```python
try:
    data = json.loads(text)
    ...
except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:
    raise LibraryError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err
```

A tuple of classes after `except` catches any of them. The words **`from err`** at the end [[keep the original error attached|raise-from]] as the cause, so if this ever shows up as a traceback, you still see what really went wrong inside.

**Watch out:** loading by putting the parsed dicts straight into `self.books`. Everything seems to work, until some code asks for `book.title` on what is really a dict, and gets `AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'title'`. Always rebuild real `Book`, `Member` and `Loan` objects when you load.

::: context memory-vs-disk Memory forgets, the disk remembers
A running program keeps its variables in the computer's **memory** (RAM). It is very fast, but it is cleared when the program ends or the power goes off. A **file** on a disk or SSD survives both, but it can only hold bytes, such as text.

So saving always means turning objects into text or bytes, and loading means turning them back. Spacecraft have the same split: fast working memory for the running software, and non-volatile storage for anything that must survive a reboot, such as logs and settings.
:::

::: context json-history Where JSON came from
JSON stands for **JavaScript Object Notation**. It grew out of the way the JavaScript language writes objects, and Douglas Crockford described it and gave it its name in the early 2000s. It is now an official standard.

It won because it is small: six kinds of value, a few pieces of punctuation, and nothing else. Web services send JSON to each other, programs keep their settings in it, and scientific and space tools use it too. Almost every language has a JSON reader built in or one install away.
:::

::: context iso-dates Why the year comes first
`2026-03-15` is the international standard way to write a date, from a standard called **ISO 8601**. Biggest unit first: year, then month, then day, each padded with zeros.

That order has a lovely property: sorting the text A to Z also sorts the dates from earliest to latest, because the most important part is compared first. `"2026-03-15" < "2026-11-02"` is `True`, and it is the right answer. It also avoids the confusion over whether `03/04` means 3 April or 4 March. Mission logs and spacecraft telemetry use this style for exactly those reasons.
:::

::: context format-promise A file format is a promise
Once you have saved files in some format, you have made a promise: files from today must still load next year. If a later version renames `"copies"` to `"count"`, every old save file breaks.

Real programs handle this by writing a version number into the file, such as `"version": 1`, and teaching the loader to read old versions too. You do not need that yet, but it is why the shape of the file deserves a moment's thought before the first save.
:::

::: context stable-diffs Comparing two saves
Engineers often keep data files in Git, which shows the lines that changed between two versions. If the keys came out in a different order every time, every save would look completely changed, even when only one book was added.

With `sort_keys=True` and `indent=2`, one new book adds a few lines and changes nothing else, so the difference between two saves shows exactly what really changed. Stable output also makes tests easy: two saves of the same data are the same string.
:::

::: context cls-not-self Why cls and not Library
Inside a class method, `cls` is whichever class the method was called on. Writing `cls()` instead of `Library()` matters when someone makes a subclass later. If `class BranchLibrary(Library)` inherits `from_json`, then `BranchLibrary.from_json(text)` builds a `BranchLibrary`, because `cls` is `BranchLibrary` in that call.

Alternative constructors are everywhere in Python's standard library: `date.fromisoformat`, `dict.fromkeys`, `int.from_bytes`. The name usually starts with "from", which is a good hint for yours.
:::

::: context round-trip There and back again
A **round trip** sends data out and brings it back, then checks nothing was lost on the way.

```svg
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 360 130" font-family="Inter, Arial, sans-serif">
  <rect x="10" y="40" width="90" height="44" rx="6" fill="#8fb8f0" stroke="#1f2a44"/>
  <text x="55" y="66" font-size="13" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">lib</text>
  <rect x="135" y="40" width="90" height="44" rx="6" fill="#ffffff" stroke="#1f2a44"/>
  <text x="180" y="66" font-size="13" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">JSON text</text>
  <rect x="260" y="40" width="90" height="44" rx="6" fill="#8fb8f0" stroke="#1f2a44"/>
  <text x="305" y="66" font-size="13" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">new lib</text>
  <line x1="100" y1="62" x2="127" y2="62" stroke="#1f2a44" stroke-width="2"/>
  <polygon points="134,62 124,57 124,67" fill="#1f2a44"/>
  <line x1="225" y1="62" x2="252" y2="62" stroke="#1f2a44" stroke-width="2"/>
  <polygon points="259,62 249,57 249,67" fill="#1f2a44"/>
  <text x="117" y="32" font-size="11" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">to_json</text>
  <text x="242" y="32" font-size="11" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">from_json</text>
  <path d="M55 84 Q55 118 180 118 Q305 118 305 84" fill="none" stroke="#b4232c" stroke-width="2" stroke-dasharray="5 4"/>
  <text x="180" y="112" font-size="12" fill="#b4232c" text-anchor="middle">must behave the same</text>
</svg>
```

A second test goes the other way: save the new library again, and the text must match the first save exactly. Stable output from step 4 makes that possible.
:::

::: context trust-boundary Where trusted data ends
A **trust boundary** is any place where data enters your program from somewhere you do not control: a file, the keyboard, the network. Inside the boundary, your own methods made the data and it follows your rules. At the boundary, anything can arrive.

So the boundary is where you check. Spacecraft software does this with every command uplinked from the ground: before acting on one, it checks the message is complete and correctly formed, and it rejects anything else. A save file deserves the same suspicion.
:::

::: context raise-from What "from err" keeps
`raise NewError(...) from err` stores the old error on the new one, as its `__cause__`. If the new error is never caught, Python prints both tracebacks, joined by the line "The above exception was the direct cause of the following exception:".

```python
try:
    Library.from_json("{}")
except LibraryError as e:
    print(e)                # corrupt save file: 'books'
    print(repr(e.__cause__))  # KeyError('books')
```

So the caller gets one simple error to catch, and whoever is debugging still sees the real reason underneath.
:::
--- task
Add saving and loading to `Library`:

- `to_json()` returns `json.dumps(data, indent=2, sort_keys=True)`, where `data` is `{"books": [...], "members": [...], "loans": [...]}`. Books and members are dicts of their fields (in the order they were added), and each loan is `{"member_id": ..., "isbn": ..., "due": "YYYY-MM-DD"}`.
- The class method `Library.from_json(text)` builds a new `Library` with real `Book`, `Member` and `Loan` objects (with `date` due dates) and returns it. Broken JSON, missing keys and bad fields raise `LibraryError`.
--- starter
# Library system, step 2: loans and rules.
from dataclasses import dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def find_by_author(self, text):
        text = text.lower()
        return sorted(b.title for b in self.books.values() if text in b.author.lower())

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def return_book(self, member_id, isbn):
        for i, loan in enumerate(self.loans):
            if loan.member_id == member_id and loan.isbn == isbn:
                return self.loans.pop(i)
        raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} has not borrowed {isbn}")
--- solution
# Library system, step 3: saving and loading.
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def find_by_author(self, text):
        text = text.lower()
        return sorted(b.title for b in self.books.values() if text in b.author.lower())

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def return_book(self, member_id, isbn):
        for i, loan in enumerate(self.loans):
            if loan.member_id == member_id and loan.isbn == isbn:
                return self.loans.pop(i)
        raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} has not borrowed {isbn}")

    def to_json(self):
        data = {
            "books": [asdict(b) for b in self.books.values()],
            "members": [asdict(m) for m in self.members.values()],
            "loans": [{"member_id": l.member_id, "isbn": l.isbn, "due": l.due.isoformat()} for l in self.loans],
        }
        return json.dumps(data, indent=2, sort_keys=True)

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, text):
        try:
            data = json.loads(text)
            library = cls()
            for b in data["books"]:
                library.add_book(Book(**b))
            for m in data["members"]:
                library.add_member(Member(**m))
            for l in data["loans"]:
                library.loans.append(Loan(l["member_id"], l["isbn"], date.fromisoformat(l["due"])))
        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:
            raise LibraryError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err
        return library
--- hint
Start with `to_json`. Add `import json` and put `asdict` in the `from dataclasses import` line. Books and members are `[asdict(b) for b in self.books.values()]` and the same for members. For loans, build each dict yourself, so that the date becomes `loan.due.isoformat()`.
--- hint
`from_json` has `@classmethod` above it and takes `(cls, text)`. Inside: `data = json.loads(text)`, `library = cls()`, then `library.add_book(Book(**b))` for each `b` in `data["books"]`, `library.add_member(Member(**m))` for each member, and `library.loans.append(Loan(l["member_id"], l["isbn"], date.fromisoformat(l["due"])))` for each loan. Return `library` at the end.
--- hint
Put everything from `json.loads` to the last loan inside `try:`, then `except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:` and `raise LibraryError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err`.
--- check case | to_json writes the agreed format
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "Dune", "Frank Herbert", 2)), lib.add_member(Member("m1", "Ada")), lib.checkout("m1", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), __import__("json").loads(lib.to_json()))[-1])(Library())
=> {"books": [{"isbn": "1", "title": "Dune", "author": "Frank Herbert", "copies": 2}], "members": [{"member_id": "m1", "name": "Ada"}], "loans": [{"member_id": "m1", "isbn": "1", "due": "2026-03-15"}]}
--- check test | to_json is stable: sorted keys and indented
(lambda lib: (lib.add_member(Member("m1", "Ada")), lib.to_json())[1] == '{\n  "books": [],\n  "loans": [],\n  "members": [\n    {\n      "member_id": "m1",\n      "name": "Ada"\n    }\n  ]\n}')(Library())
--- check test | A round trip gives back real objects
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "Dune", "F", 2)), lib.add_member(Member("m1", "Ada")), lib.checkout("m1", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), (lambda new: new.book("1") == Book("1", "Dune", "F", 2) and new.member("m1") == Member("m1", "Ada") and new.loans == [Loan("m1", "1", date(2026, 3, 15))] and new.available("1") == 1)(Library.from_json(lib.to_json())))[-1])(Library())
--- check test | Saving what you loaded gives the same text
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("9", "Emma", "Austen")), lib.add_member(Member("x", "Xi")), lib.checkout("x", "9", date(2026, 12, 25)), Library.from_json(lib.to_json()).to_json() == lib.to_json())[-1])(Library())
--- check test | from_json is a class method
isinstance(Library.__dict__["from_json"], classmethod)
--- check test | Corrupt files raise LibraryError
all(raises(LibraryError, lambda t=t: Library.from_json(t)) for t in ["not json", "{}", '{"books": [{"isbn": "1"}], "members": [], "loans": []}', '{"books": [], "members": [], "loans": [{"member_id": "a", "isbn": "1", "due": "someday"}]}'])

+++ practice | Saving the app's settings
--- task
An app keeps its settings in a dataclass `Settings` with `name` (str), `volume` (int, default `5`) and `muted` (bool, default `False`). Write the dataclass and two functions:

- `save(settings)` returns the settings as JSON text, a dict of the fields with the keys sorted (no indentation). For example, `save(Settings("ada"))` is `'{"muted": false, "name": "ada", "volume": 5}'`.
- `load(text)` reads that text back and returns a real `Settings` object. A field missing from the text takes its default, so `load('{"name": "bo"}')` is `Settings("bo", 5, False)`.
--- starter
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


@dataclass
class Settings:
    name: str
    volume: int = 5
    muted: bool = False


def save(settings):
    return str(settings)


def load(text):
    return json.loads(text)
--- solution
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


@dataclass
class Settings:
    name: str
    volume: int = 5
    muted: bool = False


def save(settings):
    return json.dumps(asdict(settings), sort_keys=True)


def load(text):
    return Settings(**json.loads(text))
--- hint
JSON cannot hold a dataclass, but it can hold a dict. `asdict` turns the dataclass into a dict of its fields; `json.dumps(..., sort_keys=True)` writes it with the keys A to Z.
--- hint
Going back, `json.loads` gives a dict, and `Settings(**d)` spreads it into keyword arguments. Fields missing from the dict are not passed, so they take their defaults.
--- check case | save writes sorted JSON
save(Settings("ada"))
=> '{"muted": false, "name": "ada", "volume": 5}'
--- check test | load gives back a real Settings
load(save(Settings("lin", 11, True))) == Settings("lin", 11, True) and type(load(save(Settings("x")))) is Settings
--- check case | Missing fields take their defaults
load('{"name": "bo"}')
=> Settings("bo", 5, False)

+++ practice | A calendar of events
--- task
Write two functions that save and load a calendar: a dict from each event's name to its `date`.

- `save_events(events)` returns JSON text: a dict from each name to its date written as `"YYYY-MM-DD"`, with `indent=2` and the keys sorted.
- `load_events(text)` reads that text and returns the dict, with real `date` objects again.

For example, `save_events({"launch": date(2026, 9, 3)})` is `'{\n  "launch": "2026-09-03"\n}'`, and an empty calendar saves as `"{}"`.
--- starter
import json
from datetime import date


def save_events(events):
    return json.dumps(events)


def load_events(text):
    return json.loads(text)
--- solution
import json
from datetime import date


def save_events(events):
    as_text = {name: day.isoformat() for name, day in events.items()}
    return json.dumps(as_text, indent=2, sort_keys=True)


def load_events(text):
    return {name: date.fromisoformat(day) for name, day in json.loads(text).items()}
--- hint
`json.dumps` cannot write a `date`, so turn each date into text first. A dict comprehension does the whole calendar in one line.
--- hint
Out: `day.isoformat()`. In: `date.fromisoformat(text)`. Pass `indent=2, sort_keys=True` to `json.dumps` so the text is the same every time.
--- check case | Saved text, sorted and indented
save_events({"splashdown": date(2026, 9, 17), "launch": date(2026, 9, 3)})
=> '{\n  "launch": "2026-09-03",\n  "splashdown": "2026-09-17"\n}'
--- check test | Loading gives real dates back
(lambda cal: load_events(save_events(cal)) == cal and type(load_events(save_events(cal))["launch"]) is date)({"launch": date(2026, 12, 31), "dock": date(2027, 1, 2)})
--- check test | An empty calendar
save_events({}) == "{}" and load_events("{}") == {}

+++ practice | Save the expense ledger
--- task
The starter has `Expense` and `Ledger` from the expense tracker. Give the ledger saving and loading:

- `to_json()` returns `json.dumps({"expenses": [...]}, indent=2, sort_keys=True)`, where each expense is a dict of its fields, in the order they were added.
- The class method `Ledger.from_json(text)` builds and returns a new ledger, adding each expense with `add`.
- `from_json` raises `ValueError` with a message starting `"corrupt save file: "` for broken JSON, a missing key, missing or unknown fields, or an amount that is not a whole number (`int`) above zero. `True` is not an amount, even though Python treats it as 1.
--- starter
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


@dataclass
class Expense:
    amount: int
    category: str
    note: str = ""


class Ledger:
    def __init__(self):
        self.expenses = []

    def add(self, amount, category, note=""):
        expense = Expense(amount, category, note)
        self.expenses.append(expense)
        return expense

    def total(self):
        return sum(e.amount for e in self.expenses)
--- solution
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


@dataclass
class Expense:
    amount: int
    category: str
    note: str = ""


class Ledger:
    def __init__(self):
        self.expenses = []

    def add(self, amount, category, note=""):
        expense = Expense(amount, category, note)
        self.expenses.append(expense)
        return expense

    def total(self):
        return sum(e.amount for e in self.expenses)

    def to_json(self):
        return json.dumps({"expenses": [asdict(e) for e in self.expenses]}, indent=2, sort_keys=True)

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, text):
        try:
            ledger = cls()
            for item in json.loads(text)["expenses"]:
                expense = Expense(**item)
                if type(expense.amount) is not int or expense.amount <= 0:
                    raise ValueError(f"bad amount: {expense.amount!r}")
                ledger.add(expense.amount, expense.category, expense.note)
        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:
            raise ValueError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err
        return ledger
--- hint
`to_json` is one line: a dict with one key, whose value is a list of `asdict(e)` for each expense. `from_json` is a class method: `@classmethod` above it, `cls` as its first parameter, and `cls()` makes the new ledger.
--- hint
Rebuild each expense with `Expense(**item)`, which raises `TypeError` for missing or unknown fields. Then check the amount: `type(x) is not int` is true for a float, a string and `True`, while `isinstance(True, int)` would let `True` through.
--- hint
Put everything from `json.loads` to the last `add` inside one `try`, and turn `ValueError`, `KeyError` and `TypeError` into `ValueError(f"corrupt save file: {err}")`, with `from err`.
--- check case | to_json writes the agreed format
(lambda l: (l.add(1250, "food", "lunch"), l.to_json())[-1])(Ledger())
=> '{\n  "expenses": [\n    {\n      "amount": 1250,\n      "category": "food",\n      "note": "lunch"\n    }\n  ]\n}'
--- check test | A round trip gives the same ledger
(lambda l: (l.add(1250, "food", "lunch"), l.add(320, "bus"), (lambda new: new.expenses == l.expenses and new.total() == 1570 and new.to_json() == l.to_json())(Ledger.from_json(l.to_json())))[-1])(Ledger())
--- check test | from_json is a class method
isinstance(Ledger.__dict__["from_json"], classmethod) and Ledger.from_json('{"expenses": []}').expenses == []
--- check test | Corrupt files raise ValueError saying so
all((lambda g: (exec("try:\n    Ledger.from_json(t)\nexcept ValueError as e:\n    msg = str(e)", g), g.get("msg", ""))[1])(dict(globals(), t=t)).startswith("corrupt save file: ") for t in ["{oops", "{}", '{"expenses": [{"amount": 5}]}', '{"expenses": [{"amount": 5, "category": "x", "tip": 1}]}', '{"expenses": [{"amount": 12.5, "category": "x"}]}', '{"expenses": [{"amount": -5, "category": "x"}]}', '{"expenses": [{"amount": "100", "category": "x"}]}', '{"expenses": [{"amount": true, "category": "x"}]}'])

+++ practice | A high-score file you cannot trust
--- task
A game saves high scores as JSON: an object from each player's name to their score, like `{"ada": 120, "bo": 0}`. Players edit the file by hand, so it cannot be trusted. Write `class ScoreError(Exception)` and `load_scores(text)`, which returns the scores as a dict, or raises `ScoreError` when the file is not valid:

- The text must be valid JSON, and the top level must be an object (a dict). An empty object gives `{}`.
- Every score must be a whole number (`int`), zero or more.
- Not valid: broken JSON, a list at the top level, a score that is a string (`"5"`), a float (`5.0`), negative, `null`, or `true` / `false`. Careful: in Python `True` counts as an `int`, so the obvious check lets it through.
--- starter
import json


class ScoreError(Exception):
    pass


def load_scores(text):
    return json.loads(text)
--- solution
import json


class ScoreError(Exception):
    pass


def load_scores(text):
    try:
        data = json.loads(text)
    except ValueError as err:
        raise ScoreError(f"bad save: {err}") from err
    if not isinstance(data, dict):
        raise ScoreError("bad save: the top level must be an object")
    for name, score in data.items():
        if type(score) is not int or score < 0:
            raise ScoreError(f"bad save: the score for {name} is {score!r}")
    return data
--- hint
Three separate things can be wrong: the text is not JSON, the top level is not a dict, or a score is not a whole number of zero or more. Check them in that order.
--- hint
`isinstance(True, int)` is `True`, because `bool` is a kind of `int` in Python. `type(score) is not int` asks for exactly an `int`, so it refuses booleans (and floats and strings).
--- check test | Valid files
load_scores('{"ada": 120, "bo": 0}') == {"ada": 120, "bo": 0} and load_scores("{}") == {}
--- check test | Broken JSON, and the wrong top level
all(raises(ScoreError, lambda t=t: load_scores(t)) for t in ["{ada: 1}", "", "[1, 2]", '"ada"', "7"])
--- check test | Scores that are not whole numbers of zero or more
all(raises(ScoreError, lambda t=t: load_scores(t)) for t in ['{"a": "5"}', '{"a": 5.0}', '{"a": -1}', '{"a": null}', '{"a": true}', '{"a": 3, "b": false}'])

+++ practice | Debug: the dates that stayed text
--- task
A travel app saves trips as JSON and loads them back. A `Trip` has a `name`, a `start` date and a number of `nights`, and `back_on()` returns the date the trip ends. Saving works: each trip is written with its date as `"YYYY-MM-DD"`.

But after `load_trips`, `back_on()` crashes with `TypeError`, and a loaded trip is not equal to the one that was saved. Fix `load_trips` so it gives back real trips.
--- starter
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date, timedelta


@dataclass
class Trip:
    name: str
    start: date
    nights: int

    def back_on(self):
        return self.start + timedelta(days=self.nights)


def save_trips(trips):
    return json.dumps([{"name": t.name, "start": t.start.isoformat(), "nights": t.nights} for t in trips])


def load_trips(text):
    return [Trip(**d) for d in json.loads(text)]
--- solution
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import date, timedelta


@dataclass
class Trip:
    name: str
    start: date
    nights: int

    def back_on(self):
        return self.start + timedelta(days=self.nights)


def save_trips(trips):
    return json.dumps([{"name": t.name, "start": t.start.isoformat(), "nights": t.nights} for t in trips])


def load_trips(text):
    return [Trip(d["name"], date.fromisoformat(d["start"]), d["nights"]) for d in json.loads(text)]
--- hint
Print `type(load_trips(text)[0].start)`. JSON has no dates, so what did `json.loads` give you for `"start"`?
--- hint
Rebuild the date on the way in with `date.fromisoformat(...)`, the reverse of `isoformat()`, and build each `Trip` from the three fields yourself.
--- check test | A loaded trip equals the saved one
(lambda trips: load_trips(save_trips(trips)) == trips)([Trip("Lisbon", date(2026, 7, 1), 5), Trip("Oslo", date(2026, 12, 28), 7)])
--- check test | back_on works after loading
load_trips(save_trips([Trip("Oslo", date(2026, 12, 28), 7)]))[0].back_on() == date(2027, 1, 4)
--- check test | No trips
load_trips(save_trips([])) == []

+++ practice | Reading old save files
--- task
The first version of a library app saved its books as a plain JSON list of `[isbn, title]` pairs. The second version saves a dict with a version number:

```
{"books": [{"copies": 2, "isbn": "1", "title": "Dune"}], "version": 2}
```

A program must read files from both versions. Write `class LibraryError(Exception)`, a dataclass `Book(isbn, title, copies=1)`, and:

- `save_books(books)`: always writes version 2, `{"version": 2, "books": [...]}`, each book a dict of its fields, with `indent=2` and the keys sorted.
- `load_books(text)`: returns a list of `Book`s. A JSON list is a version 1 file, and each book has 1 copy. A dict must have `"version": 2`; any other version raises `LibraryError(f"unsupported version: {version}")`.
- Anything else wrong (broken JSON, missing keys, a pair that is not two items, missing or unknown fields) raises `LibraryError` with a message starting `"corrupt save file: "`.
--- starter
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    copies: int = 1


def save_books(books):
    return json.dumps([asdict(b) for b in books])


def load_books(text):
    return [Book(**b) for b in json.loads(text)]
--- solution
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    copies: int = 1


def save_books(books):
    return json.dumps({"version": 2, "books": [asdict(b) for b in books]}, indent=2, sort_keys=True)


def load_books(text):
    try:
        data = json.loads(text)
        if isinstance(data, list):
            return [Book(isbn, title) for isbn, title in data]
        version = data["version"]
        if version != 2:
            raise LibraryError(f"unsupported version: {version}")
        return [Book(**b) for b in data["books"]]
    except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:
        raise LibraryError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err
--- hint
Look at the shape before anything else: `isinstance(data, list)` tells an old file from a new one. An old pair unpacks with `for isbn, title in data`, which raises `ValueError` for a pair of the wrong length.
--- hint
Raising `LibraryError` for an unknown version inside the `try` is fine: the `except` only catches `ValueError`, `KeyError` and `TypeError`, so your own error passes straight through it.
--- check test | A version 1 file
load_books('[["1", "Dune"], ["2", "Emma"]]') == [Book("1", "Dune", 1), Book("2", "Emma", 1)]
--- check test | Version 2: saved and loaded back
(lambda books: load_books(save_books(books)) == books and save_books(books) == '{\n  "books": [\n    {\n      "copies": 2,\n      "isbn": "1",\n      "title": "Dune"\n    }\n  ],\n  "version": 2\n}')([Book("1", "Dune", 2)])
--- check case | An unknown version
(lambda g: (exec("try:\n    load_books('{\"version\": 3, \"books\": []}')\nexcept LibraryError as e:\n    msg = str(e)", g), g.get("msg"))[1])(dict(globals()))
=> "unsupported version: 3"
--- check test | Corrupt files
all((lambda g: (exec("try:\n    load_books(t)\nexcept LibraryError as e:\n    msg = str(e)", g), g.get("msg", ""))[1])(dict(globals(), t=t)).startswith("corrupt save file: ") for t in ["[oops", '[["1"]]', '{"books": []}', '{"version": 2}', '{"version": 2, "books": [{"isbn": "1"}]}', '{"version": 2, "books": [{"isbn": "1", "title": "D", "pages": 3}]}', "42"])

=== pyp-12 | Library system 4: overdue loans and fines
--- teach
Last lesson the library learned to save itself as JSON and load itself back, and to refuse a damaged save file with one clear `LibraryError`. This last lesson adds what a librarian needs every morning: which books are overdue, what each member owes, and which books are due back soon. All three are built on one question about dates, so that comes first.

**Step 1: subtracting dates.** In lesson 2 you added a `timedelta` to a date to get a new date. It works the other way too: one date minus another gives a `timedelta`, the length of time between them. Its **`.days`** is that length as a whole number of days. If the second date is later than the first, `.days` is negative:

```python
from datetime import date

print((date(2026, 3, 20) - date(2026, 3, 15)).days)    # 5    five days late
print((date(2026, 3, 10) - date(2026, 3, 15)).days)    # -5   not due yet
```

Read `today - loan.due` as "how far past the due date is today?".

**Step 2: late days are never negative.** A loan that is not due yet is not "minus five days late"; it is just not late. And a book returned *on* its due date is not late either, because the difference is 0. Only positive differences count. `max(0, days)` says "the late days, but never below zero" in one step, because `max` picks the bigger of the two:

```python
max(0, 5)      # 5
max(0, -5)     # 0
```

**Step 3: one helper, used everywhere.** "How many days late is this loan?" is needed by the overdue list *and* by the fine. Write it once, as a method `days_late(loan, today)`, and call it from both. Then the two features are guaranteed to agree. Two slightly different copies of the same calculation, one with `>` and one with `>=`, is exactly how an [[off-by-one|off-by-one]] disagreement creeps in.

**Step 4: the overdue report, sorted on purpose.** `overdue(today)` gives one tuple per late loan: `(member name, book title, days late)`. The loan only stores ids, so you look the names up, as you learned in lesson 2: `self.members[loan.member_id].name` and `self.books[loan.isbn].title`.

A report is most useful with the worst cases first. Then, when two loans are equally late, by name, then by title, so that the same data [[always gives the same order|tie-breaks]]. That is the sort key trick from the sorting lesson: a tuple key, with a minus sign on the number you want largest-first:

```python
rows = [("Bo", "Emma", 1), ("Ada", "Dune", 5), ("Bo", "Ulysses", 5)]
print(sorted(rows, key=lambda r: (-r[2], r[0], r[1])))
# [('Ada', 'Dune', 5), ('Bo', 'Ulysses', 5), ('Bo', 'Emma', 1)]
```

`r[2]` is the days late, `r[0]` the name, `r[1]` the title. Ada and Bo are both 5 days late, so the name breaks the tie.

**Step 5: fines in [[cents|cents-again]], again.** The fine is 20 cents for each day each book is late. Store it as a constant, `FINE_PER_DAY = 20`, and keep the total as a whole number of cents, like the expense tracker did. Turning cents into "$1.40" is the interface's job, not the library's.

A worked example: Ada has two books. One was due on 15 March and the other on 18 March. On 20 March the first is 5 days late and the second 2 days late. That is 5 + 2 = 7 late days, and 7 × 20 = 140 cents. `fine` is the sum of `days_late(loan, today) * FINE_PER_DAY` over the member's loans, and `loans_of` already raises for an unknown member.

**Step 6: due soon.** `due_soon(today, within=3)` lists the books that come back in the next few days, so the library can send reminders. Here you want the *opposite* subtraction, `loan.due - today`: how many days until the due date. A loan counts if that number is from 0 (due today) up to `within`, [[both ends included|inclusive-window]]. Python can write that as one chained comparison:

```python
0 <= (loan.due - today).days <= within
```

Sort the matching loans soonest first, by the tuple `(loan.due, title)`, and return just the titles. Dates compare the way you would hope: an earlier date is smaller.

**Step 7: today stays an argument.** Every one of these methods depends on the date, and every one takes `today`. That is why testing them is easy: you can check "three days after the due date, the fine is 60" without [[waiting three days|fast-forward]].

**Watch out:** the direction of the subtraction. `today - loan.due` is positive when a loan is *late*; `loan.due - today` is positive when it is *not due yet*. Swap them and every late book looks early. When unsure, try one date you can check in your head.

With this lesson the library system is complete: a domain model with rules, saving and loading with a stable file format and a check on the way in, and reports that depend on time. And every part of it was tested without touching a real disk or a real clock.

::: context off-by-one Off by one day
An **off-by-one error** is a count that is wrong by exactly one: counting the posts of a fence instead of the gaps, or treating the due date itself as a late day. Here is how `max(0, (today - due).days)` counts around a due date of 15 March:

```svg
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 360 120" font-family="Inter, Arial, sans-serif">
  <line x1="20" y1="50" x2="340" y2="50" stroke="#1f2a44" stroke-width="2"/>
  <g stroke="#1f2a44" stroke-width="2">
    <line x1="40" y1="44" x2="40" y2="56"/><line x1="90" y1="44" x2="90" y2="56"/>
    <line x1="140" y1="40" x2="140" y2="60"/><line x1="190" y1="44" x2="190" y2="56"/>
    <line x1="240" y1="44" x2="240" y2="56"/><line x1="290" y1="44" x2="290" y2="56"/>
  </g>
  <g font-size="12" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">
    <text x="40" y="34">13 Mar</text><text x="90" y="34">14 Mar</text>
    <text x="140" y="34" font-weight="700">15 Mar</text><text x="190" y="34">16 Mar</text>
    <text x="240" y="34">17 Mar</text><text x="290" y="34">18 Mar</text>
  </g>
  <g font-size="13" text-anchor="middle">
    <text x="40" y="78" fill="#6c7a93">0</text><text x="90" y="78" fill="#6c7a93">0</text>
    <text x="140" y="78" fill="#6c7a93">0</text><text x="190" y="78" fill="#b4232c">1</text>
    <text x="240" y="78" fill="#b4232c">2</text><text x="290" y="78" fill="#b4232c">3</text>
  </g>
  <text x="140" y="104" font-size="11" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">due date: not late</text>
  <text x="250" y="104" font-size="11" fill="#b4232c" text-anchor="middle">days late</text>
</svg>
```

Off-by-one errors are among the most common bugs of all, which is why the rule lives in one helper.
:::

::: context tie-breaks Why ties need a rule
If you sorted only by days late, two loans that are both 5 days late could come out in either order, depending on the order the loans happen to be in the list. Run the report twice and the lines might swap.

Adding the name and then the title as tie-breakers means every possible pair has a fixed order. The same data always prints the same report, which makes it easy to test, easy to compare with yesterday's, and less confusing for the person reading it.
:::

::: context cents-again Why money stays in whole cents
The expense tracker stored money as whole cents because decimal fractions are not stored exactly as floats: `0.1 + 0.2` gives `0.30000000000000004`, not `0.3`. Add up thousands of fines as floats and the total can end up a fraction of a cent off.

Whole numbers of cents are always exact, and `140` cents can be shown as "$1.40" at the very last moment, when it is printed. It is the same idea as keeping one unit throughout a calculation, which spacecraft engineers learned the hard way when a mix-up between pound-seconds and newton-seconds helped cause the loss of the Mars Climate Orbiter in 1999.
:::

::: context inclusive-window A window with both ends included
With `today` on 12 March and `within=3`, the window runs from 12 March to 15 March, and both ends count.

```svg
<svg xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" viewBox="0 0 360 110" font-family="Inter, Arial, sans-serif">
  <rect x="45" y="36" width="210" height="28" rx="4" fill="#8fb8f0"/>
  <line x1="20" y1="50" x2="340" y2="50" stroke="#1f2a44" stroke-width="2"/>
  <g fill="#1f2a44">
    <circle cx="60" cy="50" r="5"/><circle cx="120" cy="50" r="5"/><circle cx="180" cy="50" r="5"/><circle cx="240" cy="50" r="5"/>
  </g>
  <circle cx="300" cy="50" r="5" fill="#ffffff" stroke="#6c7a93" stroke-width="2"/>
  <g font-size="12" fill="#1f2a44" text-anchor="middle">
    <text x="60" y="28">12 Mar</text><text x="120" y="28">13</text><text x="180" y="28">14</text><text x="240" y="28">15 Mar</text><text x="300" y="28">16</text>
    <text x="60" y="84">0</text><text x="120" y="84">1</text><text x="180" y="84">2</text><text x="240" y="84">3</text>
  </g>
  <text x="300" y="84" font-size="12" fill="#6c7a93" text-anchor="middle">4</text>
  <text x="150" y="104" font-size="11" fill="#1d6fd1" text-anchor="middle">due in 0 to 3 days: listed</text>
  <text x="300" y="104" font-size="11" fill="#6c7a93" text-anchor="middle">not yet</text>
</svg>
```

The numbers underneath are `(loan.due - today).days`. A loan due today has 0 and is listed; one already late has a negative number and is left out, because the overdue report covers it.
:::

::: context fast-forward Testing next week, today
Because `today` is an argument, a test can jump to any date it likes: the due date, the day after, a year later. Real systems are tested this way too. Flight software is run in simulators where the clock is controlled by the test, so a team can jump straight to the moment before a planned engine burn, or run a whole day's schedule in minutes.

The expert course's design lesson called this injecting a dependency. Your library never once asks the computer what day it is. Only the thin outer shell of a real program would call `date.today()` and pass the answer in.
:::
--- task
Add the constant `FINE_PER_DAY = 20` (in cents) and these `Library` methods:

- `days_late(loan, today)`: how many days after its due date `today` is, never negative.
- `overdue(today)`: a list of `(member name, book title, days late)` tuples, one for every loan that is late, sorted most days late first, then by name, then by title.
- `fine(member_id, today)`: the member's total fine in cents: `FINE_PER_DAY` for each day each of their loans is late. An unknown member raises `LibraryError`.
- `due_soon(today, within=3)`: the titles of the books on loan that are due between `today` and `within` days after it (both ends included), soonest first, then by title.
--- starter
# Library system, step 3: saving and loading.
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def find_by_author(self, text):
        text = text.lower()
        return sorted(b.title for b in self.books.values() if text in b.author.lower())

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def return_book(self, member_id, isbn):
        for i, loan in enumerate(self.loans):
            if loan.member_id == member_id and loan.isbn == isbn:
                return self.loans.pop(i)
        raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} has not borrowed {isbn}")

    def to_json(self):
        data = {
            "books": [asdict(b) for b in self.books.values()],
            "members": [asdict(m) for m in self.members.values()],
            "loans": [{"member_id": l.member_id, "isbn": l.isbn, "due": l.due.isoformat()} for l in self.loans],
        }
        return json.dumps(data, indent=2, sort_keys=True)

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, text):
        try:
            data = json.loads(text)
            library = cls()
            for b in data["books"]:
                library.add_book(Book(**b))
            for m in data["members"]:
                library.add_member(Member(**m))
            for l in data["loans"]:
                library.loans.append(Loan(l["member_id"], l["isbn"], date.fromisoformat(l["due"])))
        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:
            raise LibraryError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err
        return library
--- solution
# Library system, step 4: overdue loans and fines.
import json
from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass, replace
from datetime import date, timedelta

LOAN_DAYS = 14
MAX_LOANS = 3
FINE_PER_DAY = 20  # cents


class LibraryError(Exception):
    pass


@dataclass
class Book:
    isbn: str
    title: str
    author: str
    copies: int = 1


@dataclass
class Member:
    member_id: str
    name: str


@dataclass
class Loan:
    member_id: str
    isbn: str
    due: date


class Library:
    def __init__(self):
        self.books = {}
        self.members = {}
        self.loans = []

    def add_book(self, book):
        if book.copies < 1:
            raise LibraryError("a book needs at least one copy")
        if book.isbn in self.books:
            self.books[book.isbn].copies += book.copies
        else:
            self.books[book.isbn] = replace(book)
        return self.books[book.isbn]

    def add_member(self, member):
        if member.member_id in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"member {member.member_id} already exists")
        self.members[member.member_id] = member

    def book(self, isbn):
        if isbn not in self.books:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such book: {isbn}")
        return self.books[isbn]

    def member(self, member_id):
        if member_id not in self.members:
            raise LibraryError(f"no such member: {member_id}")
        return self.members[member_id]

    def find_by_author(self, text):
        text = text.lower()
        return sorted(b.title for b in self.books.values() if text in b.author.lower())

    def available(self, isbn):
        book = self.book(isbn)
        return book.copies - sum(1 for loan in self.loans if loan.isbn == isbn)

    def loans_of(self, member_id):
        self.member(member_id)
        return [loan for loan in self.loans if loan.member_id == member_id]

    def checkout(self, member_id, isbn, today):
        current = self.loans_of(member_id)
        self.book(isbn)
        if any(loan.isbn == isbn for loan in current):
            raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} already has {isbn}")
        if len(current) >= MAX_LOANS:
            raise LibraryError("loan limit reached")
        if self.available(isbn) < 1:
            raise LibraryError(f"no copies of {isbn} available")
        loan = Loan(member_id, isbn, today + timedelta(days=LOAN_DAYS))
        self.loans.append(loan)
        return loan

    def return_book(self, member_id, isbn):
        for i, loan in enumerate(self.loans):
            if loan.member_id == member_id and loan.isbn == isbn:
                return self.loans.pop(i)
        raise LibraryError(f"{member_id} has not borrowed {isbn}")

    def to_json(self):
        data = {
            "books": [asdict(b) for b in self.books.values()],
            "members": [asdict(m) for m in self.members.values()],
            "loans": [{"member_id": l.member_id, "isbn": l.isbn, "due": l.due.isoformat()} for l in self.loans],
        }
        return json.dumps(data, indent=2, sort_keys=True)

    @classmethod
    def from_json(cls, text):
        try:
            data = json.loads(text)
            library = cls()
            for b in data["books"]:
                library.add_book(Book(**b))
            for m in data["members"]:
                library.add_member(Member(**m))
            for l in data["loans"]:
                library.loans.append(Loan(l["member_id"], l["isbn"], date.fromisoformat(l["due"])))
        except (ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as err:
            raise LibraryError(f"corrupt save file: {err}") from err
        return library

    def days_late(self, loan, today):
        return max(0, (today - loan.due).days)

    def overdue(self, today):
        rows = [
            (self.members[l.member_id].name, self.books[l.isbn].title, self.days_late(l, today))
            for l in self.loans
            if self.days_late(l, today) > 0
        ]
        return sorted(rows, key=lambda r: (-r[2], r[0], r[1]))

    def fine(self, member_id, today):
        return sum(self.days_late(l, today) * FINE_PER_DAY for l in self.loans_of(member_id))

    def due_soon(self, today, within=3):
        soon = [l for l in self.loans if 0 <= (l.due - today).days <= within]
        return [self.books[l.isbn].title for l in sorted(soon, key=lambda l: (l.due, self.books[l.isbn].title))]
--- hint
Start with the helper from step 2: `days_late` returns `max(0, (today - loan.due).days)`. Everything else calls it.
--- hint
`overdue` builds a tuple `(self.members[l.member_id].name, self.books[l.isbn].title, self.days_late(l, today))` for each loan `l` in `self.loans` where `self.days_late(l, today) > 0`, then returns them sorted with `key=lambda r: (-r[2], r[0], r[1])`. `fine` is `sum(self.days_late(l, today) * FINE_PER_DAY for l in self.loans_of(member_id))`.
--- hint
For `due_soon`, keep the loans where `0 <= (l.due - today).days <= within`, sort them with `key=lambda l: (l.due, self.books[l.isbn].title)`, and return a list of their titles.
--- check test | days_late is never negative
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "Dune", "F")), lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), [lib.days_late(lib.loans[0], date(2026, 3, d)) for d in (10, 15, 16, 20)])[-1] == [0, 0, 1, 5])(Library())
--- check case | overdue lists the worst first
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, t, "A")) for i, t in (("1", "Dune"), ("2", "Emma"), ("3", "Ulysses"))], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.add_member(Member("b", "Bo")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.checkout("b", "2", date(2026, 3, 5)), lib.checkout("b", "3", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.overdue(date(2026, 3, 20)))[-1])(Library())
=> [("Ada", "Dune", 5), ("Bo", "Ulysses", 5), ("Bo", "Emma", 1)]
--- check case | Nothing overdue
(lambda lib: (lib.add_book(Book("1", "Dune", "F")), lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.overdue(date(2026, 3, 15)))[-1])(Library())
=> []
--- check case | fine adds up every late day of every book
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, i, "A")) for i in "123"], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.checkout("a", "2", date(2026, 3, 4)), lib.checkout("a", "3", date(2026, 3, 30)), lib.fine("a", date(2026, 3, 20)))[-1])(Library())
=> 140
--- check test | fine for an unknown member raises LibraryError
raises(LibraryError, lambda: Library().fine("zz", date(2026, 1, 1)))
--- check case | due_soon, soonest first, inclusive
(lambda lib: ([lib.add_book(Book(i, t, "A")) for i, t in (("1", "Dune"), ("2", "Emma"), ("3", "Ulysses"), ("4", "Beloved"))], lib.add_member(Member("a", "Ada")), lib.add_member(Member("b", "Bo")), lib.checkout("a", "1", date(2026, 3, 3)), lib.checkout("a", "2", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.checkout("b", "3", date(2026, 3, 10)), lib.checkout("b", "4", date(2026, 3, 1)), lib.due_soon(date(2026, 3, 14)))[-1])(Library())
=> ["Beloved", "Emma", "Dune"]

+++ practice | A countdown
--- task
Write `countdown(name, event, today)`, where `event` and `today` are dates. It returns a line that says how far away the event is:

- in the future: `"launch: in 5 days"`, or `"launch: in 1 day"` for exactly one day;
- on the day: `"launch: today"`;
- in the past: `"launch: 3 days ago"`, or `"launch: 1 day ago"`.
--- starter
from datetime import date


def countdown(name, event, today):
    return f"{name}: in {(today - event).days} days"
--- solution
from datetime import date


def countdown(name, event, today):
    days = (event - today).days
    if days == 0:
        return f"{name}: today"
    unit = "day" if abs(days) == 1 else "days"
    if days > 0:
        return f"{name}: in {days} {unit}"
    return f"{name}: {-days} {unit} ago"
--- hint
`(event - today).days` is how many days until the event: positive before it, 0 on the day, negative after it.
--- hint
Handle 0 first. Then pick the word: `"day"` when the number of days, ignoring its sign (`abs`), is 1, and `"days"` otherwise. A past event shows `-days`, so the number is positive.
--- check case | Future events
[countdown("launch", date(2026, 3, 20), date(2026, 3, 15)), countdown("dock", date(2026, 3, 1), date(2026, 2, 28))]
=> ["launch: in 5 days", "dock: in 1 day"]
--- check case | Today
countdown("launch", date(2026, 3, 15), date(2026, 3, 15))
=> "launch: today"
--- check case | Past events, across a year
[countdown("launch", date(2025, 12, 30), date(2026, 1, 2)), countdown("burn", date(2026, 3, 14), date(2026, 3, 15))]
=> ["launch: 3 days ago", "burn: 1 day ago"]

+++ practice | Members with late books
--- task
Write `overdue_members(loans, today)`. `loans` is a list of `(member, due)` pairs, where `due` is a date, and a member can have several loans. Return the members who have at least one loan that is late (its due date is before `today`), sorted, each name once.

A loan due today is not late yet. No late loans gives `[]`.
--- starter
from datetime import date


def overdue_members(loans, today):
    return [member for member, due in loans if due <= today]
--- solution
from datetime import date


def overdue_members(loans, today):
    return sorted({member for member, due in loans if (today - due).days > 0})
--- hint
A loan is late when `(today - due).days` is more than 0, the same test the lesson's `days_late` makes.
--- hint
A set comprehension keeps each member once; `sorted` turns it into a list in order.
--- check case | Two members late, one of them twice
overdue_members([("bo", date(2026, 3, 1)), ("ada", date(2026, 3, 10)), ("bo", date(2026, 3, 5)), ("cy", date(2026, 3, 30))], date(2026, 3, 20))
=> ["ada", "bo"]
--- check case | Due today is not late
overdue_members([("ada", date(2026, 3, 20))], date(2026, 3, 20))
=> []
--- check case | No loans
